codex
Finding D3-R1-01: Unhashed graph fields can be modified and propagated while retaining the original graphId
  category: 1. Authoritative graph boundary
  evidence: `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:229-274` hashes only the D2 ID projection, while `:1044-1057` and `:1169-1175` consume unhashed `cannotConclude`, `limitations`, and `provenance`. Reproduction changed freshness, `cannotConclude`, and `provenance.sanitizedCount` on an authentic graph without changing graphId; D3 returned `valid:true`, propagated `["CANNOT_CONCLUDE"]`, and emitted sanitizedCount `777777`.
  proposed closure: Validate the complete D2 graph schema and invariants, and bind every consumed field into an authenticated snapshot identity. A publicly recomputable diagnostic hash alone cannot prove D2 origin.
  severity: BLOCK

Finding D3-R1-02: Duplicate node IDs can count as multiple independent evidence units
  category: 2. Correlation double-count
  evidence: `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:653-680` counts every unmatched occurrence as a solo unit, while `:368-385` never rejects duplicate node IDs or malformed correlation membership. A recomputed-hash graph containing node `a` twice and no correlation groups was accepted with supporting IDs `["a","a"]`, `soloSupportCount:2`, and confidence raised to `moderate/60`.
  proposed closure: Revalidate D2 uniqueness, semantic-deduplication, correlation partition, membership completeness, canonical group IDs, and independence weights before evaluation.
  severity: BLOCK

Finding D3-R1-03: Authentic D2 contradiction edges originating at matched evidence are ignored
  category: 3. Contradiction suppression
  evidence: `contracts/r3.0d/evidence-node-contract.js:67-68` defines `contradictingEdges` as other evidence contradicting the node; D2 materializes it as `from=node, to=other` at `renderer/js/r3-0d-evidence-graph.js:1183-1188`. D3 only counts edges whose `to` is matched at `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:634-648`. Reproduction produced `{from:"a",to:"b",kind:"contradicts"}`, but hypothesis `a` had `contradictingEvidenceIds:[]`, status `supported`, score `30`.
  proposed closure: Normalize the edge semantics and count the endpoint representing “other evidence contradicting this matched node”; add a genuine D2→D3 contradiction test.
  severity: BLOCK

Finding D3-R1-04: Fabricated imported-summary evidence can be elevated to Measured
  category: 5. Credibility laundering / 12. Imported summary elevation
  evidence: D2 rejects this combination at `renderer/js/r3-0d-evidence-graph.js:1046-1049`, but D3 does not repeat that invariant before `_aggregateCredibility` at `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:689-702`. A graph with `sourceId:"imported_summary"` and `credibility:"measured"` plus a correctly recomputed graphId was accepted and emitted `credibility:"Measured"`.
  proposed closure: Revalidate every node using the D1/D2 contracts and explicitly enforce the imported-summary elevation prohibition.
  severity: BLOCK

Finding D3-R1-05: D3 is not freshness-aware and accepts arbitrarily stale evidence unchanged
  category: 10. Stale graph
  evidence: `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:704-708` hard-codes `staleContribution = 0` and states there is no freshness check. Freshness is also excluded from `_projectIdPayload` at `:231-237`. Replacing freshness with `1900-01-01T00:00:00Z` retained graphId and returned `valid:true`. This contradicts `governance/r3.0d/checkpoints/D3.json:42`.
  proposed closure: Validate freshness shape and enforce the directive’s age policy using an injected reference clock; deterministically downweight or block stale evidence.
  severity: BLOCK

Finding D3-R1-06: Node lapId mismatches are accepted
  category: 11. Case/session mismatch
  evidence: `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:368-385` checks caseId and sessionId only. A recomputed-hash graph with case lap `L1` and node lap `L2` returned `valid:true`. D2’s required rule is at `renderer/js/r3-0d-evidence-graph.js:1043-1045`.
  proposed closure: Apply D2’s lap rule verbatim: when both association and node lap IDs are non-null, require equality.
  severity: BLOCK

Finding D3-R1-07: Clone-time accessors execute before the original graph is structurally audited
  category: 15. Self-restoring replacements / 1. Authoritative graph boundary
  evidence: D3 invokes `HI.safeStructuredClone(gIn)` at `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:304-310`, then audits only the clone at `:321-325`. The HI contract explicitly warns at `contracts/r3.0d/hardened-intrinsics.js:382-385` that structuredClone invokes input traps and requires an original-shape audit first. A shallow-frozen graph can therefore contain nested getters that execute and disappear into clone data properties.
  proposed closure: Perform descriptor-only recursive shape/accessor/symbol validation on the original before cloning, then audit the clone again.
  severity: BLOCK

Finding D3-R1-08: Ambient Object.isFrozen is directly callable and bypasses the frozen-input requirement
  category: 14. Ambient intrinsic calls
  evidence: Direct ambient calls survive at `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:66`, `:93`, and `:173`. Rebinding `Object.isFrozen = () => true` caused a completely mutable authentic graph clone to pass and return `valid:true`. Section J mutates `Object.freeze`, not `Object.isFrozen`.
  proposed closure: Add a captured `safeObjectIsFrozen` HI wrapper and use it everywhere, with regression and ambient-rebinding tests.
  severity: BLOCK

Finding D3-R1-09: Section J does not mutation-protect every HI wrapper called by D3
  category: 16. HI wrapper coverage / 17. Mutation-test effectiveness
  evidence: D3 calls wrappers including `safeArrayForEach`, `safeArraySlice`, `safeGetOwnDescriptor`, `safeHasOwn`, `safeDefineDataProperty`, `safeObjectCreateNull`, `safeRegExpTest`, `safeStructuredClone`, `safeUtf8ByteLength`, and `deepOriginalShapeAudit`; Section J at `tests/r3.0d-hypothesis-engine.test.js:525-633` covers only ten ambient bindings. For example, regressing `HI.safeArraySlice` at production lines `911-937` to `.slice()` is not exercised under a hostile `Array.prototype.slice`, so that mutant is not forced to EXIT 1.
  proposed closure: Add one structurally effective source-regression/ambient-rebinding test for every runtime HI wrapper and verify each mutant independently fails the suite.
  severity: BLOCK

Finding D3-R1-10: The post-clock integrity guard does not detect most ambient mutations
  category: 19. Hostile clock
  evidence: `_intrinsicsIntact` at `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:86-95` checks only whether the HI API object appears frozen. Consequently, a clock rebinding `Array.prototype.push` is not detected at `:1163-1166`, although captured wrappers prevent that specific mutation from corrupting local state.
  proposed closure: Either extend the guard to verify captured ambient identities or narrow the documented claim to HI API integrity and rely explicitly on captured wrappers.
  severity: NIT

Finding D3-R1-11: ENVELOPE_BYTE_CAP measures a reduced preview rather than the returned envelope
  category: 20. Oversized envelope
  evidence: The check at `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:1142-1157` includes hypothesis IDs and correctly rejects only values strictly greater than the cap, but omits final `caseAssociation`, `sessionAssociation`, `provenance`, `createdAt`, `generationToken`, and `contextVersion` added at `:1178-1198`.
  proposed closure: Measure the exact final envelope immediately before freezing/returning, excluding only fields explicitly exempted by contract.
  severity: NIT

The remaining categories—4, 6, 7, 8, 9, 13, and 18—showed no independent defect: scores remain bounded integers; hidden top-level confidence keys are rejected; emitted i18n strings are registry-controlled; output arrays are sorted; the registry/API are frozen; raw observation params/source freshness are not emitted; and ordinary exceptions collapse to the structured blocked result. Category 19’s clock mutation does not currently corrupt local state, but its guard claim is overstated as noted above.

FINAL VERDICT: BLOCK

   233	  function _hash32(s) {
   234	    var h = 0x811c9dc5;
   235	    var len = (typeof s === 'string') ? s.length : 0;
   236	    for (var i = 0; i < len; i++) {
   237	      h ^= HI.safeStringCharCodeAt(s, i);
   238	      h = (h + ((h << 1) + (h << 4) + (h << 7) + (h << 8) + (h << 24))) >>> 0;
   239	    }
   240	    return _toHex8(h);
   241	  }
   242	  function _hashFNV64Hex(s) {
   243	    var slen = (typeof s === 'string') ? s.length : 0;
   244	    return _hash32(s) + _hash32(s + '|' + slen);
   245	  }
   246	  // ---------- Authoritative graph verification --------------------------------------------------
   247	  // The graph payload that produced graphId in D2 (see r3-0d-evidence-graph.js Step 16, ~line
   248	  // 1308): { caseAssociation, nodes (projected), edges, correlationGroups (projected) }. To verify
   249	  // authority, we project the same fields out of the suspect graph and recompute the hash with
   250	  // the same prefix ('graphid|v1|'). Mismatch → fabrication.
   251	  function _projectIdPayload(graph) {
   252	    var projectedNodes = HI.safeArrayMap(graph.nodes, function (n) {
   253	      var idPart = {
   254	        caseId: n.identity.caseId,
   255	        sessionId: n.identity.sessionId,
   256	        lapId: n.identity.lapId == null ? null : n.identity.lapId,
   257	        sourceId: n.identity.sourceId,
   258	        sourceVersion: n.identity.sourceVersion,
   259	      };
   260	      var obsPart = {
   261	        kind: n.observation.kind,
   262	        channel: n.observation.channel == null ? null : n.observation.channel,
   263	        i18nKey: n.observation.i18nKey,
   264	        params: n.observation.params == null ? null : n.observation.params,
   265	      };
   266	      return {
   267	        nodeId: n.nodeId,
   268	        category: n.category,
   269	        credibility: n.credibility,
   270	        provenance: n.provenance,
   271	        availability: n.availability,
   272	        confidence: { state: (n.confidence && n.confidence.state) ? n.confidence.state : null },
   273	        identity: idPart,
   274	        observation: obsPart,
   275	        limitations: n.limitations,
   276	        supportingEdges: n.supportingEdges,
   277	        contradictingEdges: n.contradictingEdges,
   278	      };
   279	    });
   280	    var projectedCgs = HI.safeArrayMap(graph.correlationGroups, function (g) {
   281	      return {
   282	        correlationGroupId: g.correlationGroupId,
   283	        memberNodeIds: g.memberNodeIds,
   284	        independenceWeight: g.independenceWeight,
   285	      };
   286	    });
   287	    return {
   288	      caseAssociation: {
   289	        caseId: graph.caseAssociation.caseId,
   290	        sessionId: graph.caseAssociation.sessionId,
   291	        lapId: graph.caseAssociation.lapId == null ? null : graph.caseAssociation.lapId,
   292	      },
   293	      nodes: projectedNodes,
   294	      edges: graph.edges,
   295	      correlationGroups: projectedCgs,
   296	    };
   297	  }
   298	
   299	  function _recomputeGraphId(graph) {
   300	    var idPayload = _projectIdPayload(graph);
   301	    var serialized = HI.stableStringify(idPayload);
   302	    return 'graph_' + _hashFNV64Hex('graphid|v' + graph.schemaVersion + '|' + serialized);
   303	  }

   305	  /**
   306	   * Validate that the supplied object IS a D2 authoritative graph snapshot. Returns
   307	   * { valid:true } or { valid:false, reasonCodes:[...] }.
   308	   *
   309	   * Checks (in order):
   310	   *  - top-level plain-object + deep-frozen
   311	   *  - schemaVersion 1
   312	   *  - required top-level keys present
   313	   *  - graphId starts with 'graph_' + 16 hex
   314	   *  - caseAssociation valid
   315	   *  - sessionAssociation.sessionId === caseAssociation.sessionId
   316	   *  - nodes/edges/correlationGroups are frozen arrays
   317	   *  - node/edge caps not exceeded
   318	   *  - recomputed graphId === asserted graphId
   319	   *  - cross-case: every node.identity.caseId === graph.caseAssociation.caseId
   320	   *  - cross-session: every node.identity.sessionId === graph.caseAssociation.sessionId
   321	   */
   322	  function _validateAuthoritativeGraph(gIn, opts) {
   323	    if (!_isPlainObject(gIn)) {
   324	      return { valid: false, reasonCodes: [CODES.EVIDENCE_NODE_INVALID] };
   325	    }
   740	  ]);

   742	  // ---------- Per-rule evaluation ---------------------------------------------------------------
   743	  function _findMatchingNodesForRule(graph, rule) {
   744	    var matched = [];
   745	    for (var i = 0; i < graph.nodes.length; i++) {
   746	      var n = graph.nodes[i];
   747	      if (HI.safeArrayIndexOf(rule.triggerEvidenceCategories, n.category) === -1) continue;
   748	      if (HI.safeArrayIndexOf(rule.triggerKinds, n.observation.kind) === -1) continue;
   749	      if (HI.safeArrayIndexOf(rule.allowedCredibility, n.credibility) === -1) continue;
   750	      _arrPush(matched, n);
   751	    }
   752	    return matched;
   753	  }
   754	
   755	  function _findContradictionsForMatched(graph, matched) {
   756	    // Codex D3 R1 RN-03 closure: D2 emits contradiction edges as `{from: node, to: other}` where
   757	    // `from` is the node whose contradictingEdges array referenced `other`. So edges where
   758	    // `from === matched.nodeId AND kind === 'contradicts'` are the contradictions of a matched
   759	    // node, and the contradictor is `edge.to`. We also accept the reverse direction for
   760	    // robustness (some future producer may emit canonical low→high form).
   761	    var matchedIds = _newSet();
   762	    HI.safeArrayForEach(matched, function (n) { _setAdd(matchedIds, n.nodeId); });
   763	    var contradicting = [];
   764	    var seenContra = _newSet();
   765	    for (var i = 0; i < graph.edges.length; i++) {
   766	      var e = graph.edges[i];
   767	      if (e.kind !== 'contradicts') continue;
   768	      var contraId = null;
   769	      if (_setHas(matchedIds, e.from) && !_setHas(matchedIds, e.to)) {
   770	        contraId = e.to;
   771	      } else if (_setHas(matchedIds, e.to) && !_setHas(matchedIds, e.from)) {
   772	        // Reverse-direction fallback for symmetry.
   773	        contraId = e.from;
   774	      } else if (_setHas(matchedIds, e.from) && _setHas(matchedIds, e.to)) {
   775	        // Both endpoints matched — pick the non-self side (matched contradicts another matched).
   776	        // Skip if same nodeId (self-edge, defensive — D2 disallows but defend anyway).
   777	        if (e.from !== e.to) contraId = e.to;
   778	      }
   779	      if (contraId === null) continue;
   780	      if (_setHas(seenContra, contraId)) continue;
   781	      _setAdd(seenContra, contraId);
   782	      _arrPush(contradicting, contraId);
   783	    }
   784	    HI.safeArraySort(contradicting, _strcmp);
   785	    return contradicting;
   786	  }
   787	
   788	  function _countIndependentGroups(matched, graph) {
   789	    // Map nodeId → correlationGroupId (a node may belong to at most one correlation group). A node
   790	    // not in any correlation group counts as its own independent unit.
   791	    var memberToGroupId = _newSet();
   792	    for (var gi = 0; gi < graph.correlationGroups.length; gi++) {
   793	      var g = graph.correlationGroups[gi];
   794	      for (var mi = 0; mi < g.memberNodeIds.length; mi++) {
   795	        HI.safeDefineDataProperty(memberToGroupId, g.memberNodeIds[mi], g.correlationGroupId);
   796	      }
   797	    }
   798	    var groupSeen = _newSet();
   799	    var soloCount = 0;
   800	    var matchedGroupIds = [];
   801	    for (var i = 0; i < matched.length; i++) {
   802	      var nid = matched[i].nodeId;
   803	      var d = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(memberToGroupId, nid);
   804	      if (d && typeof d.value === 'string') {
   805	        var gid = d.value;
   806	        if (!_setHas(groupSeen, gid)) {
   807	          _setAdd(groupSeen, gid);
   808	          _arrPush(matchedGroupIds, gid);
   809	        }
   810	      } else {
   811	        soloCount += 1;
   812	      }
   813	    }
   814	    HI.safeArraySort(matchedGroupIds, _strcmp);
   815	    return { groupIds: matchedGroupIds, soloCount: soloCount, total: matchedGroupIds.length + soloCount };
   816	  }
   817	
   818	  function _credibilityWeightFor(c) {
   819	    if (c === 'measured') return 5;
   820	    if (c === 'derived') return 3;
   821	    if (c === 'heuristic') return 1;
   822	    return 0;
   823	  }
   824	  function _aggregateCredibility(matched) {
   825	    var hasMeasured = false, hasDerived = false, hasHeuristic = false, hasSynthetic = false;
   500	    deduplicationSummary: g.deduplicationSummary, correlationGroups: g.correlationGroups,
   501	    limitations: g.limitations, cannotConclude: g.cannotConclude,
   502	    provenance: g.provenance, createdAt: g.createdAt,
   503	    generationToken: g.generationToken, contextVersion: g.contextVersion,
   504	  });
   505	  var r = HE.buildHypothesisSet({ graph: tampered });
   506	  chk('H8: tampered graphId rejected as forged',
   507	    r.eligible === false
   508	      && HI.safeArrayIndexOf(r.reasonCodes, RC.REASON_CODES.HYPOTHESIS_AUTHORITY_FORGED) !== -1);
   509	})();

   514	// RN-01: tampered cannotConclude / limitations / provenance MUST NOT propagate
   515	(function () {
   516	  var g = _buildGraph([_baseNode({})]);
   517	  // Build a fake graph with hash-valid hypothetical projection but caller-injected
   518	  // non-hashed fields. (We can't easily reproduce the hash forge here; we instead assert
   519	  // that even if a caller fabricates a graph whose graphId we recompute correctly, our
   520	  // engine ignores the non-hashed fields and re-derives them.)
   521	  // Take the authentic graph and assert that the snapshot output does NOT echo
   522	  // graph.cannotConclude/limitations/provenance.sanitizedCount values.
   523	  var r = HE.buildHypothesisSet({ graph: g });
   524	  chk('HR01-1: snapshot.provenance.sourceGraphSanitizedCount equals graph.nodes.length (not graph.provenance.sanitizedCount which is non-hashed)',
   525	    r.hypothesisSet.provenance.sourceGraphSanitizedCount === g.nodes.length);
   526	  // Build a tampered shell where we mutate graph.provenance.sanitizedCount to a bogus value
   527	  // and verify it does NOT appear in the output. Since graph is deep-frozen we can't
   528	  // mutate; instead we construct a fresh structurally-similar object.
   529	  var tamperedShell = Object.freeze({
   530	    schemaVersion: g.schemaVersion, graphId: g.graphId,
   531	    caseAssociation: g.caseAssociation, sessionAssociation: g.sessionAssociation,
   532	    nodes: g.nodes, edges: g.edges, topologicalOrder: g.topologicalOrder,
   533	    deduplicationSummary: g.deduplicationSummary, correlationGroups: g.correlationGroups,
   534	    limitations: Object.freeze(['LIMITATION_FAKE_INJECT']),  // not a real reason code anyway
   535	    cannotConclude: Object.freeze(['FAKE_REASON_INJECT']),
   536	    provenance: Object.freeze({ builderVersion: 999, inputCount: 99999, sanitizedCount: 777777, rejectedCount: 0, rejectedReasonsSummary: Object.freeze({}) }),
   537	    createdAt: g.createdAt, generationToken: g.generationToken, contextVersion: g.contextVersion,
   538	  });
   539	  var r2 = HE.buildHypothesisSet({ graph: tamperedShell });
   540	  // The tamperedShell is NOT frozen-equivalent to g (different object identity for limitations etc.)
   541	  // but the hashed projection matches — D3 must still process it but ignore the tampered fields.
   542	  if (r2.valid === true) {
   543	    chk('HR01-2: tampered shell with valid graphId does NOT propagate fake "FAKE_REASON_INJECT" into snapshot.cannotConclude',
   544	      HI.safeArrayIndexOf(r2.hypothesisSet.cannotConclude, 'FAKE_REASON_INJECT') === -1);
   545	    chk('HR01-3: tampered shell does NOT propagate "LIMITATION_FAKE_INJECT"',
   546	      HI.safeArrayIndexOf(r2.hypothesisSet.limitations, 'LIMITATION_FAKE_INJECT') === -1);
   547	    chk('HR01-4: tampered sanitizedCount=777777 NOT propagated; re-derived from nodes.length',
   548	      r2.hypothesisSet.provenance.sourceGraphSanitizedCount === g.nodes.length);
   549	  } else {
   550	    // If authority rejected the tampered shell entirely (also acceptable), still satisfies the
   551	    // closure intent: caller cannot smuggle fake fields into D3 output.
   552	    chk('HR01-2: tampered shell rejected (alternative acceptable closure)', r2.eligible === false);
   553	    chk('HR01-3: tampered shell rejected (alternative acceptable closure)', r2.eligible === false);
   554	    chk('HR01-4: tampered shell rejected (alternative acceptable closure)', r2.eligible === false);
   555	  }
   556	})();

   558	// RN-02: duplicate nodeId rejected
   559	(function () {
   560	  // We can't easily construct a "valid-hash + duplicate-nodeId" graph without recomputing the
   561	  // hash. Easier: directly test the engine on a hand-crafted graph where we know D3's invariant
   562	  // check is what rejects it (not D2's dedup). We'll bypass D2 by constructing a frozen plain
   563	  // object that recomputes its graphId correctly using D3's hash math (which mirrors D2's).
   564	  // Trick: just take an authentic graph and verify the invariant check fires on a duplicate.
   565	  // Since the authentic D2 graph has no duplicates, we craft a fabricated graph and ensure
   566	  // duplicate-node case is rejected (even if authority-forged happens first, the test confirms
   567	  // some form of rejection — the desired behavior).
   568	  var n = _baseNode({});
   569	  var g = _buildGraph([n]);
   570	  // Construct a frozen shell with duplicate nodeIds. graphId won't match recomputed → forged.
   571	  var dupShell = Object.freeze({
   572	    schemaVersion: 1,
   573	    graphId: g.graphId,
   574	    caseAssociation: g.caseAssociation,
   575	    sessionAssociation: g.sessionAssociation,
   576	    nodes: Object.freeze([g.nodes[0], g.nodes[0]]),  // same node twice
   577	    edges: Object.freeze([]),
   578	    topologicalOrder: Object.freeze([g.nodes[0].nodeId]),
   579	    deduplicationSummary: g.deduplicationSummary,
   580	    correlationGroups: Object.freeze([]),
   581	    limitations: g.limitations,
   582	    cannotConclude: g.cannotConclude,
   583	    provenance: g.provenance,
   584	    createdAt: g.createdAt,
   585	    generationToken: g.generationToken,
   586	    contextVersion: g.contextVersion,
   587	  });
   588	  var r = HE.buildHypothesisSet({ graph: dupShell });
   589	  chk('HR02-1: duplicate nodeId rejected (forged OR explicit duplicate)',
   590	    r.eligible === false);
   591	})();

   718	// ---------- Section J — Mutation coverage (HI wrappers used by D3) -------------------------------
   719	// We probe the engine's reliance on HI by rebinding ambient prototypes; the engine MUST use captured
   720	// references and not be affected. The harness uses its own captured Object.defineProperty + Reflect.apply
   721	// to avoid self-pollution.
   722	console.log('Section J — HI wrapper mutation resilience');
   723	var _origObjectDefineProperty = Object.defineProperty;
   724	var _origReflectApply = Reflect.apply;
   725	function _runRebind(container, key, hostileImpl, action) {
   726	  var orig = container[key];
   727	  var hits = 0;
   728	  var wrapped = function () { hits += 1; return _origReflectApply(hostileImpl, this, arguments); };
   729	  _origObjectDefineProperty(container, key, { value: wrapped, configurable: true, writable: true });
   730	  var result;
   731	  try { result = action(); }
   732	  finally {
   733	    _origObjectDefineProperty(container, key, { value: orig, configurable: true, writable: true });
   734	  }
   735	  return { hits: hits, result: result };
   736	}
   737	(function () {

   741	    return HE.buildHypothesisSet({ graph: g });
   742	  });
   743	  chk('J1: hostile Array.prototype.push never invoked by engine', r.hits === 0);
   744	  chk('J1: engine still produced valid result', r.result.valid === true);
   745	})();

The R1 closures are present, but two areas require deeper scrutiny: freshness currently has an explicit “accept without policy” test, and the pre-clone audit’s “no Proxy traps” claim depends on the hardened-intrinsics implementation. I’m tracing both against D2 semantics and the checkpoint directive before deciding severity.

     1	/**
     2	 * contracts/r3.0d/hardened-intrinsics.js — R3.0D Hardened Intrinsics Toolkit.
     3	 *
     4	 * Single source of truth for closure-captured pristine intrinsic references and the narrow set
     5	 * of safe wrappers that the rest of R3.0D's contract / runtime path is permitted to use.
     6	 *
     7	 * Codex D2 Round 15 architectural convergence (per SKYLINE directive 2026-06-29):
     8	 *
     9	 *   "只要內部不再執行ambient lookup ... 而只使用module-init捕獲、closure-private的pristine
    10	 *    references，攻擊者替換ambient method後自我還原也沒有意義，因為hostile replacement根本不會
    11	 *    被呼叫。"
    12	 *
    13	 * Rounds 1-15 demonstrated that any defence-in-depth that lets validation code call an AMBIENT
    14	 * prototype method (`arr.push`, `Object.keys`, `String(x)`, `Number.isInteger`, …) is defeated by
    15	 * a hostile replacement that self-restores on its own invocation. The integrity guard then sees
    16	 * a clean state at the next checkpoint, but the validator already used the rebound binding.
    17	 *
    18	 * This module is the FOUNDATION layer. Every other R3.0D contract + the renderer must call ONLY
    19	 * the safe wrappers exported here. Direct ambient property access (`Array.isArray`, `obj.length`,
    20	 * `JSON.stringify`, `Object.freeze`, etc.) is FORBIDDEN throughout the validation path.
    21	 *
    22	 * The wrappers themselves capture pristine intrinsic references at module-init time (BEFORE any
    23	 * caller code can run) and invoke them via captured `Reflect.apply`. They NEVER:
    24	 *   - Use `.call`, `.apply`, or `.bind` on captured methods (those go through
    25	 *     Function.prototype.* which is rebindable).
    26	 *   - Use ambient property lookup on the captured object (which would fire prototype-chain
    27	 *     accessors installed after import).
    28	 *   - Fall back to JSON-round-trip for cloning (which invokes hostile toJSON / drops Symbols
    29	 *     non-deterministically).
    30	 *   - Invoke caller toJSON / Proxy traps / accessor descriptors (`.value` reads on a
    31	 *     `_ObjectGetOwnPropertyDescriptor` result avoid `[[Get]]` semantics).
    32	 *
    33	 * Inputs that carry an accessor descriptor, a Symbol-keyed own property, a non-enumerable own
    34	 * property, a non-Object.prototype prototype, an Array subclass, a mutated Array prototype, or
    35	 * a class instance fail-closed at the structural audit (`deepOriginalShapeAudit`).
    36	 *
    37	 * UMD: Node require / Electron renderer global (R3_0D_HardenedIntrinsics).
    38	 */

   285	    if (arr === null || arr === undefined) return arr;
   286	    var args = cmp ? [cmp] : [];
   287	    var r = _apply(_ArrayPrototypeSort, arr, args);
   288	    if (r !== _SENTINEL) return r;
   289	    // Manual insertion sort fallback
   290	    var len;
   291	    try { len = arr.length; } catch (e) { return arr; }
   292	    if (typeof len !== 'number') return arr;
   293	    for (var s = 1; s < len; s++) {
   294	      var cur = arr[s];
   295	      var k = s - 1;
   296	      while (k >= 0 && (cmp ? cmp(arr[k], cur) > 0 : arr[k] > cur)) { arr[k + 1] = arr[k]; k--; }
   297	      arr[k + 1] = cur;
   298	    }
   299	    return arr;

   350	  function safeUtf8ByteLength(s) {

   361	      } catch (e) { /* fall through to manual */ }
   362	    }
   363	    var n = 0;
   364	    var len = s.length;
   365	    for (var i = 0; i < len; i++) {
   366	      var c = safeStringCharCodeAt(s, i);
   367	      if (c < 0x80) n += 1;
   368	      else if (c < 0x800) n += 2;
   369	      else if (c >= 0xDC00 && c <= 0xDFFF) n += 6; // lone low surrogate -> \uXXXX
   370	      else if (c >= 0xD800 && c <= 0xDBFF) {       // high surrogate
   371	        if (i + 1 < len) {
   372	          var c2 = safeStringCharCodeAt(s, i + 1);
   373	          if (c2 >= 0xDC00 && c2 <= 0xDFFF) { n += 4; i++; continue; } // valid pair = 4-byte UTF-8
   374	        }
   375	        n += 6; // lone high -> \uXXXX
   376	      }
   377	      else n += 3;
   378	    }
   379	    return n;
   380	  }
   381	
   382	  // safeStructuredClone(v) — captured structuredClone. Returns null on failure (DataCloneError

   389	    if (r === _SENTINEL) return null;
   390	    return r;

   419	    return r === true;

   422	  // safeIsPlainShape(v) — TOP-LEVEL (non-recursive) plain-shape classification. Returns one of:
   423	  //   'plain-object' — v is a non-null, non-array object whose prototype is exactly
   424	  //                    Object.prototype or null. Class instances are rejected.
   425	  //   'plain-array'  — v passes Array.isArray AND its prototype is exactly Array.prototype.

   435	    var proto = safeGetPrototypeOf(v);
   436	    if (isArr) {
   437	      if (proto !== _ArrayPrototype) return 'reject';
   438	      return 'plain-array';
   439	    }
   440	    if (proto === _ObjectPrototype || proto === null) return 'plain-object';
   441	    return 'reject';
   442	  }

   444	  // deepOriginalShapeAudit(v, depth) — recursive structural validator. Returns true ONLY if v
   445	  // is composed exclusively of plain objects (Object.prototype proto), plain arrays
   446	  // (Array.prototype proto, NOT subclass), strings, finite numbers, booleans, null. Rejects:
   447	  //   - class instances (non-Object.prototype proto)
   448	  //   - Array subclasses (non-Array.prototype proto)
   449	  //   - Proxies that lie about their prototype (lying still produces a wrong proto here)
   450	  //   - Symbol-keyed own properties at any depth
   451	  //   - Non-enumerable own properties at any depth
   452	  //   - Accessor descriptors at any depth
   453	  //   - Function values
   454	  //   - Infinity / NaN numbers
   455	  //   - Recursion past depth cap (32)
   456	  // Calls captured intrinsics only; the audit itself never invokes a [[Get]] on the input
   457	  // (uses descriptor reads exclusively).
   458	  function deepOriginalShapeAudit(v, depth) {
   459	    if (depth === undefined) depth = 0;
   460	    if (depth > 32) return false;
   461	    if (v === null) return true;
   462	    var t = typeof v;
   463	    if (t === 'string' || t === 'boolean') return true;
   464	    if (t === 'number') return safeNumberIsFinite(v);
   465	    if (t === 'function' || t === 'symbol' || t === 'undefined') return false;
   466	    if (t !== 'object') return false;
   467	    // prototype check
   468	    var proto = safeGetPrototypeOf(v);
   469	    var isPlainArray = false;
   470	    if (proto === null) {
   471	      // Object.create(null) — accept; treated as plain object with null proto
   472	    } else if (proto === _ObjectPrototype) {
   473	      // plain object
   474	    } else if (proto === _ArrayPrototype) {
   475	      // plain array — additional check: Array.isArray must agree
   476	      var isArr = _apply(_ArrayIsArray, _Array, [v]);
   477	      if (isArr !== true) return false;
   478	      isPlainArray = true;
   479	    } else {
   480	      return false; // class instance / Array subclass / mutated prototype
   481	    }
   482	    // structural key audit (own keys, including non-enumerable, including Symbol)
   483	    var allKeys = safeOwnKeys(v);
   484	    if (allKeys === null) return false;
   485	    var enumKeysOnly = safeKeys(v);
   486	    if (enumKeysOnly === null) return false;
   487	    // For plain Array: an extra non-enumerable own property "length" is intrinsic (every
   488	    // Array has it by spec). Accept exactly that one extra. Any OTHER non-enumerable or
   489	    // Symbol key is a rejection signal.
   490	    var allowedExtra = isPlainArray ? 1 : 0;
   491	    if (allKeys.length !== enumKeysOnly.length + allowedExtra) return false;
   492	    var len = allKeys.length;
   493	    for (var i = 0; i < len; i++) {
   494	      var k = allKeys[i];
   495	      if (typeof k === 'symbol') return false;
   496	      var d = safeGetOwnDescriptor(v, k);
   497	      if (d === null || d === undefined) return false;
   498	      if (!('value' in d)) return false; // accessor descriptor
   499	      // Allow the intrinsic Array length (non-enumerable). For all other keys, must be enumerable.
   500	      if (d.enumerable !== true) {
   501	        if (!(isPlainArray && k === 'length')) return false;
   502	      }
   503	      // Recurse into nested
   504	      var child = d.value;
   505	      if (typeof child === 'object' && child !== null) {
   506	        if (!deepOriginalShapeAudit(child, depth + 1)) return false;
   507	      }
   508	      else if (typeof child === 'number') {
   509	        if (!safeNumberIsFinite(child)) return false;
   510	      }
   511	      else if (typeof child === 'function' || typeof child === 'symbol' || typeof child === 'undefined') {
   512	        return false;
   513	      }
   514	    }
   515	    return true;
   516	  }

   518	  // safeObjectCreateNull() — return a fresh object with a NULL prototype using the captured
   519	  // Object.create reference invoked via Reflect.apply. Added 2026-06-29 for the renderer
   520	  // evidence-graph builder which uses `_ObjectCreate(null)` as a Map-shaped sentinel (proto-null
   521	  // bag where attacker-controlled nodeIds like 'constructor' / '__proto__' cannot collide with
   522	  // Object.prototype keys). Without this wrapper the caller would need its own captured
   523	  // reference, fragmenting the single-source-of-truth invariant. No-arg only — the wrapper does
   524	  // not accept a custom prototype because every R3.0D caller wants the null-proto case.
   525	  function safeObjectCreateNull() {
   526	    var r = _apply(_ObjectCreate, _Object, [null]);
   527	    if (r === _SENTINEL) return null;
   528	    return r;
   529	  }

   531	  // safeObjectAssign(target, source) — shallow-copy `source`'s own enumerable string-keyed
   532	  // properties into `target` using captured intrinsics ONLY. NEVER invokes Object.assign
   533	  // (which routes through ambient [[Get]] / Proxy traps and Object.prototype setters). Each
   534	  // copied key goes through safeDefineDataProperty so prototype setters are bypassed. Values
   535	  // are read via descriptor access (no [[Get]] trap fires). Returns target.
   536	  //
   537	  // Added 2026-06-29 for evidence-node-contract.js per-node sanitized-output construction
   538	  // (replaces `Object.freeze(Object.assign({}, n.observation.params))`). Single-purpose
   539	  // narrow helper: callers that need a more elaborate merge / multi-source assign should
   540	  // compose this with safeKeys themselves rather than expand the helper surface here.
   541	  function safeObjectAssign(target, source) {
   542	    if (target === null || target === undefined) return target;
   543	    if (source === null || source === undefined) return target;
   544	    if (typeof source !== 'object') return target;
   545	    var keys = safeKeys(source);
   546	    if (keys === null) return target;
   547	    var len = keys.length;
   548	    for (var i = 0; i < len; i++) {
   549	      var k = keys[i];
   550	      var d = safeGetOwnDescriptor(source, k);
   551	      if (d === null || d === undefined) continue;
   552	      if (!('value' in d)) continue; // accessor descriptor — skip rather than fire its getter
   553	      safeDefineDataProperty(target, k, d.value);
   554	    }
   555	    return target;
   556	  }

   558	  // deepFreeze(v) — recursive Object.freeze via captured _ObjectFreeze. Skips primitives,
   559	  // freezes both arrays and plain objects + their reachable subtree. Returns v.
   560	  function deepFreeze(v) {
   612	  // FNV-1a 32-bit hash → lowercase hex. Used ONLY as a diagnostic identifier (graphId / correlationGroupId);
   613	  // the actual dedup decisions use the FULL canonical string, not the hash, so a collision in the hash
   614	  // CANNOT cause silent data loss (RN-04 closure). Double-pass widening for nicer-looking ids.
   615	  // Char-code reads go through HI.safeStringCharCodeAt; numeric-to-hex coercion uses
   616	  // HI.safeStringCoerce together with a manual base-16 fold (no Number.prototype.toString ambient
   617	  // call). _strLastN already routes through HI.safeStringSlice.
   618	  function _toHex8(h) {
   619	    var hexChars = '0123456789abcdef';
   620	    var out = '';
   621	    var x = h >>> 0;
   622	    for (var i = 7; i >= 0; i--) {
   623	      var nibble = (x >>> (i * 4)) & 0xF;
   624	      out += hexChars[nibble];
   625	    }
   626	    return out;
   627	  }
   628	  function _hash32(s) {
   629	    var h = 0x811c9dc5;
   630	    var len = (typeof s === 'string') ? s.length : 0;
   631	    for (var i = 0; i < len; i++) {
   632	      h ^= HI.safeStringCharCodeAt(s, i);
   633	      h = (h + ((h << 1) + (h << 4) + (h << 7) + (h << 8) + (h << 24))) >>> 0;
   634	    }
   635	    return _toHex8(h);
   636	  }
   637	  function _hash64(s) {
   638	    var slen = (typeof s === 'string') ? s.length : 0;
   639	    return _hash32(s) + _hash32(s + '|' + slen);
   640	  }

+// RN-01: tampered cannotConclude / limitations / provenance MUST NOT propagate
+(function () {

+    r.hypothesisSet.provenance.sourceGraphSanitizedCount === g.nodes.length);

+  // and verify it does NOT appear in the output. Since graph is deep-frozen we can't
+  // mutate; instead we construct a fresh structurally-similar object.
+  var tamperedShell = Object.freeze({
+    schemaVersion: g.schemaVersion, graphId: g.graphId,
+    caseAssociation: g.caseAssociation, sessionAssociation: g.sessionAssociation,
+    nodes: g.nodes, edges: g.edges, topologicalOrder: g.topologicalOrder,
+    deduplicationSummary: g.deduplicationSummary, correlationGroups: g.correlationGroups,
+    limitations: Object.freeze(['LIMITATION_FAKE_INJECT']),  // not a real reason code anyway
+    cannotConclude: Object.freeze(['FAKE_REASON_INJECT']),
+    provenance: Object.freeze({ builderVersion: 999, inputCount: 99999, sanitizedCount: 777777, rejectedCount: 0, rejectedReasonsSummary: Object.freeze({}) }),
+    createdAt: g.createdAt, generationToken: g.generationToken, contextVersion: g.contextVersion,
+  });
+  var r2 = HE.buildHypothesisSet({ graph: tamperedShell });
+  // The tamperedShell is NOT frozen-equivalent to g (different object identity for limitations etc.)
+  // but the hashed projection matches — D3 must still process it but ignore the tampered fields.
+  if (r2.valid === true) {
+    chk('HR01-2: tampered shell with valid graphId does NOT propagate fake "FAKE_REASON_INJECT" into snapshot.cannotConclude',
+      HI.safeArrayIndexOf(r2.hypothesisSet.cannotConclude, 'FAKE_REASON_INJECT') === -1);

   200	  // certain shapes; fail-closed on any throw.
   201	  function _toCleanCopy(v) {
   202	    if (v === null || v === undefined) return v;
   203	    if (typeof v !== 'object') return v;
   204	    // Codex D1 R3 Finding RN-11 closure: NO JSON.stringify fallback. HI.safeStructuredClone
   205	    // returns null when structuredClone is unavailable OR when the clone throws — preserving the
   206	    // original fail-closed posture (caller treats null as "not a plain object").
   207	    return HI.safeStructuredClone(v);
   208	  }
   209	  /**
   210	   * _hasHiddenOwnKey(v) — detects Symbol-keyed OR non-enumerable own properties on the TOP-LEVEL
   211	   * of v. structuredClone silently drops both, so without this check a value like { caseId: 'x',
   212	   * [hostileSym]: payload } would clone to { caseId: 'x' } and pass validation. The pre-clone check
   213	   * rejects such inputs at the boundary so the failure surface mirrors the Codex D1 R1 RN-01
   214	   * recommended behaviour exactly. Throws (Proxy traps lying) → treat as hidden (fail closed).
   215	   *
   216	   * Refactored: safeOwnKeys returns BOTH string + symbol own keys, safeGetOwnPropertyNames returns
   217	   * all string own keys (enumerable + non-enumerable), safeKeys returns only enumerable string
   218	   * keys. (a) any symbol key in safeOwnKeys → hidden. (b) allStringNames.length !==
   219	   * enumStringKeys.length → at least one non-enumerable string own property → hidden.
   220	   * Any wrapper-null return (= Proxy/intrinsic failure) → fail-closed = hidden.
   221	   */
   222	  function _hasHiddenOwnKey(v) {
   223	    if (v === null || typeof v !== 'object') return false;
   224	    var ownKeys = HI.safeOwnKeys(v);
   225	    if (ownKeys === null) return true;
   226	    var len = ownKeys.length;
   227	    for (var i = 0; i < len; i++) {
   228	      if (typeof ownKeys[i] === 'symbol') return true;
   229	    }
   230	    var allNames = HI.safeGetOwnPropertyNames(v);
   231	    var enumKeys = HI.safeKeys(v);
   232	    if (allNames === null || enumKeys === null) return true;
   233	    if (allNames.length !== enumKeys.length) return true;
   234	    return false;
   235	  }
   236	  /**
   237	   * _isOriginalPlainObject(v) — Codex D1 R4 Finding RN-11 closure: prototype check on the ORIGINAL
   238	   * input BEFORE structuredClone runs. structuredClone happily converts an ordinary class instance
   239	   * into a plain object copy (DataCloneError only on a narrower set: functions, Symbol values,
   240	   * SharedArrayBuffer with shared:true, etc.), which means a class instance with valid own fields
   241	   * + an inherited toJSON method could otherwise pass _isPlain AFTER cloning even though the
   242	   * ORIGINAL was a class instance. Calling this check BEFORE toCleanCopy in each main validator
   243	   * makes class instances fail-closed at the boundary regardless of their own-field shape.
   244	   *
   245	   * Refactored to HI.safeIsPlainShape which performs the captured Array.isArray + captured
   246	   * getPrototypeOf check internally. Returns true only for the 'plain-object' classification
   247	   * (proto === Object.prototype OR null, non-array, non-null object).
   248	   */
   249	  function _isOriginalPlainObject(v) {
   250	    return HI.safeIsPlainShape(v) === 'plain-object';
   251	  }
   252	  /**
   253	   * _hasNonPlainNestedObject(v, depth) — Codex D1 R5 Finding RN-11 (nested-level) closure:
   254	   * recursively walks the ORIGINAL input pre-clone and returns true if ANY nested object (at any
   255	   * depth, including inside arrays) has a prototype that is not Object.prototype / null / Array.
   256	   * Catches class instances embedded as identity / confidence / observation / nested entry values.
   257	   * Also rejects accessor descriptors at any level — a hostile getter at depth would otherwise be
   258	   * invoked by structuredClone's [[Get]] semantics. Symbol keys are silently skipped here (the
   259	   * top-level hasHiddenOwnKey already rejects them at the boundary; descending into Symbol-keyed
   260	   * subtrees would itself invoke their getters). Depth cap 32 prevents pathological recursion.
   261	   *
   262	   * Refactored: HI.safeIsPlainShape does the per-level Array-subclass / class-instance / mutated-
   263	   * prototype rejection (Codex D1 R6 Finding RN-11 closure for array subclasses); HI.safeOwnKeys
   264	   * + HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor handle the per-key walk; the .value structural read still avoids
   265	   * invoking [[Get]] (so a hostile accessor at depth is never fired).
   266	   */
   267	  function _hasNonPlainNestedObject(v, depth) {
   268	    if (depth == null) depth = 0;
   269	    if (depth > 32) return true;
   270	    if (v === null || typeof v !== 'object') return false;
   271	    var shape = HI.safeIsPlainShape(v);
   272	    if (shape === 'reject') return true;
   273	    var keys = HI.safeOwnKeys(v);
   274	    if (keys === null) return true;
   275	    var len = keys.length;
   276	    for (var i = 0; i < len; i++) {
   277	      var k = keys[i];
   278	      if (typeof k === 'symbol') return true; // any nested symbol key — fail closed
   279	      var d = HI.safeGetOwnDescriptor(v, k);
   280	      if (d === null || d === undefined) return true;
   281	      if (typeof d.get === 'function' || typeof d.set === 'function') return true; // accessor descriptor
   282	      if (_hasNonPlainNestedObject(d.value, depth + 1)) return true;
   283	    }
   284	    return false;
   285	  }

/**
 * tests/r3.0d-hypothesis-engine.test.js — R3.0D D3 · Hypothesis Engine adversarial tests.
 *
 * Sections (per SKYLINE Continuous Resume Directive §7):
 *   A) Functional happy paths — valid graph → hypothesis set; multiple hypotheses; determinism.
 *   B) Structural — output schema, deep-freeze, ID stability, sort order, cap enforcement.
 *   C) Authority — graphId recomputation, deep-frozen requirement, fabricated graph rejection.
 *   D) Scope — wrong case / wrong session, future schema, stale generation token, identity forged.
 *   E) Confidence — duplicate evidence not double-counted, correlation dedup, contradiction
 *      lowers confidence, stale doesn't support, heuristic ≠ measured, low credibility floor.
 *   F) Limitations & cannotConclude — heuristic must declare LIMITATION_HEURISTIC_ONLY; synthetic
 *      must declare LIMITATION_SYNTHETIC_ONLY; empty rule set produces structured cannotConclude.
 *   G) Validation actions + alternative explanations — fixed enum, structured, linked to hyp.
 *   H) Security — Proxy / getter / accessor / Symbol key / non-enumerable / class instance /
 *      nested class / Array subclass / mutated Array.prototype / cyclic object / prototype
 *      pollution / hostile clock / oversized strings / oversized lists / oversized snapshot.
 *   I) Privacy — output contains no raw samples, no paths, no filenames, no private identifiers,
 *      no stack traces.
 *   J) Mutation coverage — each HI.* wrapper used by D3 has a mutation-protected direct test.
 *   K) HI-only audit — output structure does not depend on caller's ambient prototypes.
 *
 * Style: tests print `<message>: <ok|FAIL>` lines so the per-file manifest scanner parses

'use strict';

var HE = require('../renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js');
var EG = require('../renderer/js/r3-0d-evidence-graph.js');
var CR = require('../contracts/r3.0d/credibility-contract.js');
var HC = require('../contracts/r3.0d/hypothesis-contract.js');
var RC = require('../contracts/r3.0d/reason-codes.js');
var HI = require('../contracts/r3.0d/hardened-intrinsics.js');

var pass = 0, fail = 0;
function chk(msg, cond, detail) {
  if (cond) { pass += 1; }
  else { fail += 1; console.log('  FAIL ' + msg + (detail !== undefined ? '  ' + JSON.stringify(detail) : '')); }
}

// ---------- Fixtures -------------------------------------------------------------------------
var BASE_CLOCK = function () { return '2026-06-29T01:00:00Z'; };

function _baseIdentity(overrides) {
  var base = {
    caseId: 'case_001',
    sessionId: 'sess_001',
    lapId: null,
    sourceId: 'csv_import_v1',
    sourceVersion: '1.0',
    freshness: '2026-06-29T00:00:00Z',
  };
  if (overrides) {
    for (var k in overrides) base[k] = overrides[k];
  }
  return base;
}
function _baseNode(overrides) {
  var n = {
    schemaVersion: 1,
    nodeId: 'n_default',
    category: 'data_quality',
    identity: _baseIdentity(),
    credibility: 'measured',
    provenance: 'real',
    availability: 'available',
    confidence: { state: 'not_computed' },
    observation: { kind: 'channel_missing', channel: 'brake_pressure', i18nKey: 'r3.0d.obs.cm.bp', params: null },
    limitations: ['LIMITATION_MISSING_CHANNEL'],
    supportingEdges: [],
    contradictingEdges: [],
  };
  if (overrides) for (var k in overrides) n[k] = overrides[k];
  return n;
}
function _buildGraph(nodes, caseAss) {
  var ca = caseAss || { caseId: 'case_001', sessionId: 'sess_001', lapId: null };
  var eg = EG.buildEvidenceGraph({ caseAssociation: ca, rawEvidence: nodes }, { clock: BASE_CLOCK });
  if (!eg.valid) throw new Error('fixture build failed: ' + JSON.stringify(eg).slice(0, 200));
  return eg.graph;
}

(function () {
  // A1: single channel_missing → 1 hypothesis from rule_dq_channel_missing
  var g = _buildGraph([_baseNode({})]);
  var r = HE.buildHypothesisSet({ graph: g }, { clock: BASE_CLOCK });
  chk('A1: valid envelope returned for single channel_missing node', r.valid === true);
  chk('A1: exactly one hypothesis emitted', r.hypothesisSet && r.hypothesisSet.hypotheses.length === 1);
  chk('A1: hypothesis ruleId is rule_dq_channel_missing', r.hypothesisSet.hypotheses[0].ruleId === 'rule_dq_channel_missing');
  chk('A1: hypothesis category is data_quality', r.hypothesisSet.hypotheses[0].category === 'data_quality');
  chk('A1: status is supported (1 group, no contradiction)', r.hypothesisSet.hypotheses[0].status === 'supported');
  chk('A1: confidence.state is in CONFIDENCE_STATES', HI.safeArrayIndexOf(HE.CONFIDENCE_STATES, r.hypothesisSet.hypotheses[0].confidence.state) !== -1);
  chk('A1: confidence.score is integer in [0,100]',
    HI.safeNumberIsInteger(r.hypothesisSet.hypotheses[0].confidence.score) === true
      && r.hypothesisSet.hypotheses[0].confidence.score >= 0
      && r.hypothesisSet.hypotheses[0].confidence.score <= 100);
})();

  chk('A2: driver_behaviour hypothesis present', HI.safeArrayIndexOf(cats, 'driver_behaviour') !== -1);

(function () {
  // A3: determinism — same graph, two calls → identical hsetId, identical hypothesis IDs
  var g = _buildGraph([_baseNode({})]);
  var r1 = HE.buildHypothesisSet({ graph: g }, { clock: BASE_CLOCK });
  var r2 = HE.buildHypothesisSet({ graph: g }, { clock: function () { return '2026-06-29T02:00:00Z'; } });
  chk('A3: same graph → same hypothesisSetId', r1.hypothesisSet.hypothesisSetId === r2.hypothesisSet.hypothesisSetId);
  chk('A3: same graph → same hypothesis count', r1.hypothesisSet.hypotheses.length === r2.hypothesisSet.hypotheses.length);

    r1.hypothesisSet.hypotheses[0].hypothesisId === r2.hypothesisSet.hypotheses[0].hypothesisId);

})();

// ---------- Section B — Structural ---------------------------------------------------------------
console.log('Section B — structural');
(function () {
  var g = _buildGraph([_baseNode({})]);
  var r = HE.buildHypothesisSet({ graph: g });
  var hs = r.hypothesisSet;

  chk('B1: top-level envelope is frozen', Object.isFrozen(r) === true);
  chk('B2: hypothesisSet is frozen', Object.isFrozen(hs) === true);
  chk('B3: hypotheses array is frozen', Object.isFrozen(hs.hypotheses) === true);
  chk('B4: each hypothesis is frozen', hs.hypotheses.every(function (h) { return Object.isFrozen(h); }));
  chk('B5: validationActions array is frozen', Object.isFrozen(hs.validationActions) === true);
  chk('B6: cannotConclude is frozen', Object.isFrozen(hs.cannotConclude) === true);
  chk('B7: schemaVersion = 1', hs.schemaVersion === 1);
  chk('B8: hypothesisSetId matches hset_<16-hex>', /^hset_[0-9a-f]{16}$/.test(hs.hypothesisSetId));
  chk('B9: sourceGraphId equals input graphId', hs.sourceGraphId === g.graphId);
  chk('B10: caseAssociation mirrors graph', hs.caseAssociation.caseId === 'case_001' && hs.caseAssociation.sessionId === 'sess_001');
  chk('B11: sessionAssociation.sessionId mirrors caseAssociation.sessionId',
    hs.sessionAssociation.sessionId === hs.caseAssociation.sessionId);
  chk('B12: provenance has required keys',

codex
Finding D3-R2-01: Correlation groups can be stripped to inflate confidence
  evidence: `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:463-491,788-815` validates listed members but does not require every node to belong to its D2-derived group. Removing an authentic two-node group and its `correlated_with` edge, then independently recomputing graphId, returned `valid:true`, `soloSupportCount:2`, `moderate/60`.
  severity: BLOCK
  proposed closure: Reconstruct D2 correlation partitions from node identities and require exact group IDs, memberships, weights, and correlated edges.

Finding D3-R2-02: Claimed node-ID and weight invariants are bypassable
  evidence: `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:419-427` checks only non-empty node IDs despite `ID_RE` at `:176-178`. A hash-valid `../bad` ID was emitted in `supportingEvidenceIds`. At `:485-489`, string weight `"not-a-number"` produces `NaN`, bypasses both comparisons, and is accepted.
  severity: BLOCK
  proposed closure: Enforce ID grammar and byte limits; require finite numeric weight and exact `Object.is(weight, 1 / count)` equality.

Finding D3-R2-03: RN-05 remains open when no age policy is supplied
  evidence: `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:405-409,446-460,842` applies age rejection only when both options exist and still hard-codes freshness contribution to zero. A graph dated `2020-01-01` returned `valid:true` without options. The suite explicitly accepts this at `tests/r3.0d-hypothesis-engine.test.js:630-632`, contradicting `governance/r3.0d/checkpoints/D3.json:42,139`.
  severity: BLOCK
  proposed closure: Define a mandatory deterministic maximum age/reference time and fail closed when unavailable, or revise the directive and checkpoint contract.

Finding D3-R2-04: The pre-clone audit executes Proxy traps
  evidence: `contracts/r3.0d/hardened-intrinsics.js:456-506` calls Proxy-observable `getPrototypeOf`, `ownKeys`, `keys`, and `getOwnPropertyDescriptor`. A Proxy-wrapped authentic graph triggered 50 traps before rejection. Therefore the no-trap claim at `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:326-343` is false.
  severity: BLOCK
  proposed closure: Establish authority through a non-observable D2-minted identity/brand before reflective traversal; do not claim descriptor inspection is Proxy-trap-free.

Finding D3-R2-05: Top-level input and options retain accessor/TOCTOU surfaces
  evidence: `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:1136,1145-1156` directly reads `input.graph`, `generationToken`, and `contextVersion`; `:405-408` repeatedly reads option fields. An enumerable `input.graph` getter executed once and the request returned `valid:true`.
  severity: BLOCK
  proposed closure: Descriptor-audit and snapshot the complete input/options envelopes once before any value read; reject accessors and Proxies.

Finding D3-R2-06: Several closure tests do not exercise their claimed invariant
  evidence: `tests/r3.0d-hypothesis-engine.test.js:558-590` permits any forged rejection for RN-02; `:593-648` often stops at D2 for RN-04/RN-06; `:667-668` permits the RN-07 getter to fire; `:695-701` only measures a normal envelope. No positive contradiction-direction test exists (`:337-349` tests zero contradictions).
  severity: NIT
  proposed closure: Add hash-valid D3-boundary fixtures and assertions for the exact reason code/output behavior.

Finding D3-R2-07: Date/Object captures occur after dependency initialization
  evidence: Imports run at `renderer/js/r3-0d-hypothesis-engine.js:38-52`; captures occur at `:60-75`. Direct lexical invocation of captured `Date.parse` is safe, but the stated pre-import capture guarantee is inaccurate.
  severity: NIT
  proposed closure: Capture before imports or provide the operations through HI; narrow the initialization claim if dependency initialization is trusted.

Finding D3-R2-08: The D3 checkpoint manifest was not updated for Round 2
  evidence: `governance/r3.0d/checkpoints/D3.json:35,42,46,137-139` still describes clone-before-audit behavior, graph-level cannotConclude passthrough, 87 tests/10 wrappers, and freshness-aware scoring.
  severity: NIT
  proposed closure: Synchronize the manifest with the candidate implementation and 117-assertion suite.

R1 closure status:

- RN-01, RN-03, RN-04, RN-06, RN-08, RN-09, RN-10, RN-11: verified in implementation.
- RN-02: open via Findings 01–02.
- RN-05: open via Finding 03.
- RN-07: accessor ordering improved, but Proxy safety remains open via Finding 04.
